| `UNIT-HOVER-020` (one clause of the body: that mask bits `0x20` and `0x800` are alternatives. The `CUnit`/`CAirUnit`/`CStructure` name comparison, bits `0x1`, `0x2` and `0x4`, the exact-name-versus-derivation distinction, the `view+0x98c`/`view+0x990` stores and the seven-bit completeness this experiment closed all stand; the row that closes it and the row that carries this correction are the same one, named in the “what is true instead” cell rather than here, because this cell’s ids are read as overturned) | Verbatim: “`[0x005eb62c][obj+0x20]` decides bit **`0x800`** when its `+0x8c` is non-zero (`0041af68`) and bit **`0x20`** otherwise (`0041af81`)” | **High** (the row's confidence cell reads “High for the five bits above (each is a cited `OR` against a cited `strcmp` or `AND`, in one routine, with its literal dumped)”, and this is one of those five) | [EXP-0218](../experiments/EXP-0218-hover-cursor-cascades/) | The two bits are not alternatives. `0x0041af5c CMP [ECX+0x8c],0x0` with `0x0041af63 74 0b JZ 0041af70` sends the zero case past the `0x800` store at `0x0041af6b`; the non-zero case falls through to that store and then runs `0x0041af6e eb 0e JMP 0041af7e`, which lands ON the `0x20` store at `0x0041af84` rather than past it. So a hit `CStructure` always carries `0x20`, and carries `0x800` in addition when `+0x8c` is non-zero. A third arm exists that the overturned clause does not describe: with `+0x8c` zero and `[EBP-0x80]+0x64` non-zero, `0x0041af79 JMP 0041ad6a` skips the object and sets neither bit. `AI-CURSOR-231` carries the corrected statement, from a decode of `FUN_0041ab8e` from its prologue to its own `RET`. The consumer consequence is that a hostile `CStructure` gives the `select` cursor rather than `attack`: `0x0040d9df` tests `0x20` alone, and under the overturned clause a `CStructure` whose `+0x8c` is non-zero would carry only `0x800`, fall through, and give `attack` | **REFUTED** — the clause asserts an exclusivity the branch does not have, and a consumer that believed it draws the attack cursor over roughly the structures whose cell record carries `+0x8c` |
| `AI-CURSOR-052` (two clauses of the body: the Alt "regardless" clause and the clause that the hostility bit `0x4` appears only inside `mask & 0x24`. The headline, the `FUN_0041ab8e` hit-test description, the attack/swarm arms of the `0x0040d72b` cascade, the select-suppression effect of `mask & 0x24` at `0x0040d760`-`0x0040d773`, and the cursor-to-art binding all stand; the modifier-latch row is named in the “what is true instead” cell rather than here, because this cell's ids are read as overturned) | Verbatim: “with `[0x005eb560]` set the cursor is **move** regardless (`0040d72b`)”, and verbatim: “the hostility bit `0x4` appears only inside `mask & 0x24`, whose effect is to **suppress the select cursor** (`0040d760`…`0040d773`)” | **High** (the row's own confidence cell lifted an earlier **Medium** cap and reads “**High** throughout, therefore: the cursor-to-art binding (a PE read on both roots), each `AND` and `MOV` cited above, and now the gates themselves”; its consumer-terms sentence repeated the Alt clause as “`[0x005eb560]` held forces the move cursor”) | [EXP-0218](../experiments/EXP-0218-hover-cursor-cascades/) | `FUN_0040d1f1` contains **two** mask-to-cursor cascades, not one. `AI-CURSOR-052` read the cascade at `0x0040d72b`, which is entered only after four gates at `0x0040d55d`, `0x0040d5a5`, `0x0040d5be` and `0x0040d5d5` all pass; every one of the four jumps instead to a second cascade at `0x0040d84d`. **The Alt clause.** On the `0x0040d72b` path, `0x0040d72f JZ 0040d73b` sends the Alt-held case to `0x0040d731 MOV EAX,[0x5ef9b8]` / `0x0040d736 MOV [EBP-0x28],EAX` and then `JMP 0040d77c`. Six later stores to the same local are reachable from there and each replaces the move value: `0x0040d793` (`0x005ef9d4`, pickup, taken whenever `[EBP-0x58]` is set, because its guard at `0x0040d785` requires the local to be exactly the move slot the Alt arm just wrote), `0x0040d837` (`0x005efa14`, town), `0x0040db18` and `0x0040db58` (both storing `0x005ef9b4`, default, loaded at `0x0040db12` and `0x0040db52`), `0x0040db82` (`[sess+0x3d8]`) and `0x0040dc91` (`0x005efa20`, backpack). On the `0x0040d84d` path the Alt latch is not read at all by the first two arms (`0x0040d861` and `0x0040d898`, whose stores at `0x0040d87a`/`0x0040d884` and `0x0040d8af`/`0x0040d8ba` are reached without any `[EBP-0x20]` test); the first `CMP [EBP-0x20],0x0` on that path is at `0x0040d9cb`. **The hostility-bit clause.** The bit is tested alone at two addresses. `0x0040d7ba  83 e1 04  AND ECX,0x4` gates the cast-to-move table lookup at `0x0040d818`. `0x0040d9c4  83 e0 04  AND EAX,0x4` opens the second cascade's hostility arm, and with no modifier latch set and `mask & 0x20` clear that arm stores the **attack** cursor at `0x0040d9e6  a1 c0 f9 5e 00  MOV EAX,[0x5ef9c0]` / `0x0040d9eb  MOV [EBP-0x28],EAX`. An ordinary hover over a hostile unit with no key held therefore shows attack, which the overturned clause excluded by construction. `AI-CURSOR-226` and `AI-CURSOR-227` carry the corrected statements. `AI-KEYMOD-059`'s reading of the three modifier latches is unaffected and stands | **REFUTED** — both clauses assert something about the routine that is not so, and a consumer that believed either builds the wrong cursor: no attack cursor on a plain hostile hover, and an Alt-held move cursor where the routine shows pickup, town, default or backpack |

## What the column says

**78 of the 99 rows carried High** when believed — recomputed 2026-08-01 by [EXP-0083](../experiments/EXP-0083-patrol/) while adding four, by splitting each row on its unescaped pipes and reading the third cell, because the standing figure said "34 of the 47" against a table that already held **93** rows: it had last been recounted 2026-07-30 from the
*Confidence when believed* column itself, because the previous figure ("22 of 33") had
been carried forward by hand past twelve further rows and was stale. (The tallies in the
three paragraphs below were computed at **eighteen** rows and describe those eighteen; the
twenty-seven added since, EXP-0053's two included, are counted in this first figure only —
each row names its own overturning experiment in the last column.) Every one of them held
across the corpus at the time, and holding across the corpus is what "High" was being
awarded for.

**Fifteen of the eighteen were corpus-derived and fell to `rom.exe`** — the last two only
when the 2026-07-25 confidence sweep applied a correction the disassembly had already
made and nobody had propagated. That asymmetry is why the confidence scale now caps
corpus-only evidence at Medium ([`../METHODOLOGY.md`](../METHODOLOGY.md#confidence-scale)).

**Three of the eighteen are the same failure**, and it is not a wrong model — it is a
right number measured on a base that later moved, in an evidence file nobody re-ran:
`TERR-LIGHT-018`, then `TERR-LIGHT-016` and `ALM-TERR-015`, the last two found only by
re-reading the ledgers against each other. Note where they were hiding: `ALM-TERR-015` is
a `.reg` value table published in the **ALM** ledger, which is why EXP-0033's re-derivation
recorded `data/map.reg` as having no published table and skipped it. **When a base moves,
grep for the numbers, not for the format's own ledger.**

**The three that were not** are the more instructive group, and all three are terrain
lighting. `TERR-LIGHT-013` claimed the x87 disassembly explicitly — *"formula + all
constants read from the x87 disassembly"* — and the span was still wrong, because the
function was read but the **span was inferred** and then confirmed by a fit.
`TERR-LIGHT-018` was a statistic that a *later* correction silently invalidated:
nothing about it was ever refuted, its base moved underneath it. `TERR-LIGHT-011` read
both blitters correctly and published only **half of what they do** — the colour, not
the position — so from EXP-0023 until EXP-0035 the ledger described a renderer that
does not exist, and nothing in the corpus could ever have contradicted it. So:

- naming the function is not the same as establishing the claim from it — say **which
  instructions** carry **which part**, and mark the rest as inferred;
- **a fit that agrees is not a check.** Two different wrong gradient rules both
  reproduce 74;
- when an experiment moves a **base**, grep the ledgers for every *number* derived
  from it, not just every *claim* about it;
- **reading the routine is not the same as reading what it does.** Ask of every routine
  you cite: what does it compute *besides* the thing you came for? `TERR-LIGHT-011`
  named both blitters, quoted their inner loop, and never asked why one of them takes
  twelve arguments and the other eight.


## What EXP-0040 added to the method (2026-07-28)

The eighteenth and nineteenth entries above come from a **confidence audit** rather than from a
new question: EXP-0039 found that Ghidra creates no function for code reached only through a
vtable, and EXP-0040 asked how much of the ledger that endangered. The population — every row
asserting an exhaustive enumeration over the image — is **34 rows, 33 of them carrying a High
clause**. Nine were re-checked. **Seven confirmed, two amended, none retracted outright.**

Three things worth keeping:

- **The blind spot is a measured quantity, not an anecdote.** 6.2 % of `.text` was disassembled
  code with no containing function, and **607 of 1 875 `.rdata` code-pointer targets (32 %)** had
  no function. `CUnit` was not special. It is now repaired (`tools/ghidra/RepairVtables.java`).
- **The exposure is not uniform, and one of the two known casualties was a *writing* error.**
  An instruction-listing sweep (`ScanField imm:`/`disp:`) *sees* orphan code and prints `[?]`;
  `TERR-TILE-044`'s missing reader was in that row's own scan output and was dropped when the
  prose listed readers by name. Of the three readers EXP-0040 adds to the same row, two were
  genuinely invisible and one was not. A rule addressing only the tooling would have caught two
  of three.
- **A screen for a bad *shape* is not a verdict.** `RES-LOOKUP-023` was included as a positive
  control: it was flagged by the same screen and had already handled the blind spot correctly in
  2026, grading itself Medium for exactly this reason. It came back correct. Population
  membership describes the form of the evidence, not a suspicion — which is why the rule in
  `AGENTS.md` asks an enumeration to **state its instrument and that instrument's blind spot**,
  not to be distrusted.
| `TRIG-DROP-013` (the fallback range and the array read) | Verbatim: "**when `x * y == 0` substitutes `rand() % 0x46 + 0x1e` for both coordinates**", and the row's model of the read — one array, unpacked, with no statement that the *index* is chosen | **High**, and the clause sat inside the row's High cell beside the write, the read and the `0x44 + 0x28 = 0x6c` check that the row rightly called "the check that could have failed" | [EXP-0096](../experiments/EXP-0096-campaign-mission/) (`MISSION-DROP-002`) | `FUN_00504003` is **not** a modulo. `00504010`…`00504027` is `rand()` (0..0x7fff) × `(n+1)`, then the compiler's signed divide by 32768 (`CDQ` / `AND EDX,0x7fff` / `ADD EAX,EDX` / `SAR EAX,0xf`), which is **inclusive of `n`**: the fallback is uniform on **30..100** per axis, not 30..99, and it is not modulo-biased. The same routine also chooses the array **index** — `004d4187 CALL 0x004ae4f0` / `SUB EAX,0x1` / `004d4190 CALL 0x00504003` — so the drop cell is a **uniform random pick over the array**, and `player+0x60` is a per-player override tested before the array is read at all (`004d4136`…`004d4147`) | **CORRECTED** — the row's structure is right and its arithmetic is not. What it would cost a consumer: an off-by-one at the top of the fallback range and a wrong distribution, both invisible on shipped data because every map carries a nonzero cell; and a first-element read that is indistinguishable from the engine's on all 38 shipped maps and wrong on any authored map with two drop locations |
| `SPR16A-TXT-023` (the "no conversion anywhere" premise, and the open question resting on it) | Verbatim: "Under `char−32` with **no conversion anywhere** (`SUB AL,0x20` — SPR16A-FONT-018; **no code-page pass** — RES-NAME/EXP-0034), a CP866 string through these atlases lands on accent glyphs and blank cells. **How the RU game displays Russian text is open**: … either an unidentified path converts, or fontN text garbles, or those strings never reach `DrawText`" | **Medium**, and the row named its own lift correctly — "its resolution needs runtime observation of the RU game, which requires a writable copy and was not performed". No runtime observation was needed in the end; the missing evidence was two instructions away in the routine the row already cited | [EXP-0097](../experiments/EXP-0097-ru-text/) (`TEXT-CONV-001`, `TEXT-LANG-002`, `TEXT-FIT-004`) | **A code-page pass exists and runs on every byte of every string the engine draws.** `FUN_004562f0` maps `0x80..0xAF → +0x30` and `0xE0..0xEF → +0x10` when `[0x005eb57c] == 1`, and `SUB AL,0x20` is applied to *its result*: the instruction two before the `SUB` this row cited is `0045788e CALL 0x004562f0`. The three disjuncts resolve to the **first** — an unidentified path converts — and the encodings the row measured are all correct and all needed: the data strings really are CP866, the `README.TXT` really is CP1251, and a *second* converter `FUN_00468a90` joins them (`TEXT-IN-005`). The selector is data, not code: `rom.exe` is byte-identical across the roots and the language is the last character of the `main\id` resource, `"english 0"` versus `"russian 1"`. **What the miss cost is instructive rather than expensive**: the row's inference was sound on its premise, the premise was a *negative* — "no code-page pass" — carried over from `EXP-0034`, which had established it for archive **entry names** and not for glyph indexing, and no sweep was run to test it in the new place. A negative inherited across a change of subject is not evidence | REFUTED |
| `TRIG-END-009` (the "told once" clause, and the latch model behind it) | Verbatim: "**`player+0x3c`** … gates the report, **so each player is told once**" | **High** — the clause sat inside the row's High cell beside the counters, the reporter's tests and the two announcement ids, all of which stand | [EXP-0101](../experiments/EXP-0101-mission-end/) (`MISSION-END-013`, `MISSION-LATCH-014`) | **The two arms guard on different values of the same field, so a player is told once per OUTCOME, not once per mission.** The lose arm skips only when `+0x3c` is already **2** (`004d8c66 JZ 004d8ca1`) and the win arm only when it is already **1** (`004d8cbd JZ 004d8cf8`), so a mission won and then lost announces both, in that order, ending at 2; and the same routine's re-placement arm writes `+0x3c = 0` outright (`004d8a7b`), so the field is not a one-way latch at all. Both compares are literal `== 1` on counters that only ever rise and that nothing clears inside a mission, which is the second half of the correction: after a second increment the reporter matches neither state | REFUTED |
| `SESS-END-011` (that reaching phase 2 reaches the mission-end arm) | Verbatim: "`FUN_00473110`'s `case 0x41d` reads `campaign+0x6bc`: … **otherwise — phase 2, the campaign — the mission-end arm**", followed by the arm's contents | **High** for "the arm structure", and the omission rode inside it: a consumer building the four-way switch from this row runs the teardown, the outro and the town routing on every mission that ends | [EXP-0101](../experiments/EXP-0101-mission-end/) (`MISSION-PATH-015`) | **There is a fifth branch and it is the first instruction of the fourth.** `00473e3e CMP dword ptr [EBP + 0x3bc],EDI` / `00473e44 JZ 0x004745fe` — and `0x004745fe` is the handler arm of window message `0x41e`, the lose chain, per the message dispatch tables read out of the PE. `campaign+0x3bc` is written 1 only by the win arm of message `0x430` (`00474083`, `00474099`) and zeroed at mission start by `FUN_00477650`/`FUN_00477c00`, so everything this row describes is the **win** path; a lost mission's `0x41d` tears the session down through `FUN_00479dd0` and ends at `FUN_004769c0` and the menu. The row is right about what the arm does and wrong about when it runs | SUPERSEDED |
| `TEXT-CONV-001` (the injectivity clause, and the reason given for it) | Verbatim: "The map is **injective** on `0x80..0xFF` — the two moved blocks land on `0xB0..0xDF` and `0xF0..0xFF`, **which no unmoved byte occupies** — so **no two source bytes can collide on one glyph**" | **High**, and the row's Confidence cell said the corpus "discriminates the alternative rather than merely agreeing with this one". Confirmed in three independent instruction sequences — the roster paint routine's two loops and the hit-test routine `FUN_0047f4c0` — all three computing the identical magic-multiply-then-`IDIV` sequence. `tools/roomgeom` tabulates both formulas' divergence mechanically | **SUPERSEDED** — the grid's existence, its shared numbering across the talk-only and mercenary halves, and both loop bounds are unaffected; only the per-slot byte address is wrong from the 4th roster entry on. A consumer that used the routine name to locate the code containing either call site would open `FUN_0040eaee` and find neither address there | **REFUTED** — the routine named for two of the four call sites is not the one containing them, though the exit-set enumeration for `FUN_0040d1f1` itself, unaffected by which routine calls it, stands |
| `ALM-LIM-067` (the type-9 index-zero clause only; the seven other limits stand) | Verbatim: “The `+0x06` link is 1-based, so type-9 index 0 is not addressable from a type-8 element at all” | **Medium** (the decrement was read, but the sentence applied it in the wrong direction) | [EXP-0227](../experiments/EXP-0227-authored-enchantments-death-drops/) (`ALM-EFFLINK-072`) | Both the loader and consumer subtract one from a positive file link before indexing. File value 0 means none; file value 1 therefore addresses zero-based type-9 index 0. The shipped campaign has positive links and every one resolves under this operation | **REFUTED** — the base convention was right and its stated consequence was the opposite of what follows |
| `ALM-TRIG-049` (the type-9 tail-coordinate gloss only; the 26+6n wire shape stands) | Verbatim: the tail elements' “first two repeat the record's own tile” | **Medium** (the unique framing sweep established boundaries, then assigned the repeated-looking values a role without a consumer) | [EXP-0227](../experiments/EXP-0227-authored-enchantments-death-drops/) (`ALM-EFFREC-071`) | The consumer reads each tail as `u16 kind, u16 low, u16 high` and appends an Effect; authored kind 41 is remapped to runtime kind 49. ## ROM2 engine-sharing floor and null-population corrections

A confidence review of `EXP-2001` found the experiment's own same-binary self-collision
count was being read as a cross-binary chance-collision floor, and two downstream
arithmetic arguments built on that misreading. The correction pass added an
independent null population (`WININET.DLL` + `SHLWAPI.DLL`, unrelated Microsoft-authored
Win32 code under the same ROM2 install root) and a strict mnemHash+instrCount+byteLen
matching tier, then re-read every affected row against them.

| Claim | Former wording | Confidence when believed | Evidence | Correction | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R2-ENGINE-003` (the "these rates are the chance-collision floor a cross-binary match must clear" framing clause only; the same-binary self-collision counts by bucket and hash granularity stand) | Verbatim: "A same-binary collision is by construction not cross-binary code sharing, so these rates are the chance-collision floor a cross-binary match must clear before it says anything about ROM2" | High (a complete count over the entire non-thunk population at both hash granularities, not a sample) | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`null-control.tsv`) | An independent null population (2,200 non-thunk functions) gives the actual cross-binary false-match rate, scaled to the 18,639-function ROM2 union by `1-(1-p)^scale`: 71.45% (1–5 instr.), 1.00% (6–10), 30.01% (11–20), 1.21% (21–50), 2.60% (51–100), 2.53% (101+), pooled 1.29% at instrCount ≥ 51. Except at the shortest bucket this floor is far below the same-binary rate the retracted clause used in its place — 71.0% same-binary vs. 1.00% cross-binary at 6–10 instructions, a 71× difference. The same-binary counts remain an accurate, unchanged measurement of rom.exe's own intra-program code duplication; they are not a valid proxy for how often an unrelated binary matches rom.exe by chance | **PARTIALLY RETRACTED** — the same-binary counts stand; treating them as the cross-binary floor does not |
| `R2-ENGINE-005` (the six-bucket floor-clearance verdict list only; the raw identical/changed/absent/not-found counts and the instrCount≥51 restriction stand) | Verbatim: "Only at 21–50 instructions (34.1% vs. a 29.3% floor), 51–100 (20.9% vs. a 7.5% floor) and 101+ (8.0% identical vs. a 6.6% floor) does the observed identical rate clear its own floor by a margin" | Medium ("the floor is a same-binary proxy, not a confirmed-unrelated third binary") | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`identical-rate-by-size.tsv`) | Read against R2-ENGINE-003's corrected cross-binary floor, five of six buckets clear, not three, and the specific buckets differ: 6–10 (62.75% vs. 1.00% floor, 62.7× margin), 11–20 (62.30% vs. 30.01%, 2.08×), 21–50 (34.07% vs. 1.21%, 28.2×), 51–100 (20.94% vs. 2.60%, 8.05×), 101+ (7.96% vs. 2.53%, 3.15×) all clear; only 1–5 (27.27% vs. 71.45%) does not. The retracted list named the wrong three buckets as clearing and omitted the two (6–10, 11–20) that clear by the widest margins of all | **REFUTED** — the bucket-verdict list was read against an invalid floor and named the wrong buckets |
| `R2-ENGINE-006` (the "rules out (a) as the dominant explanation" discriminating-power conclusion only; the 109/110 synthetic-name count stands) | Verbatim: "The 99.1% synthetic-name share rules out (a) as the dominant explanation for this tier" | High ("the alternative it excludes is named and the count that excludes it is exact and complete") | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`synthetic-name-rates.tsv`) | The base rate of real names across the whole instrCount≥51 rom.exe population is already 99.69% synthetic (6 of 1,961 named); the identical tier's 99.09% synthetic share (unchanged, still 109/110) is not below this base rate by a margin that discriminates anything — a subset drawn entirely from R2-ENGINE-004's own MFC/CRT library control would be expected to show a similarly near-total synthetic share, since Ghidra's Function ID recovery named only 0.31% of the whole population regardless of a function's true origin. No comparison this experiment ran separates library-code origin from game-code origin for this tier | **REFUTED** — the comparison needed a measured base rate and used none; with the base rate measured, it discriminates nothing |
| `R2-ENGINE-007` (the "floor squared" statistical-surprise argument only; the co-occurrence descriptive counts and "not client-only" reading stand) | Verbatim: "independent chance hits landing in both named binaries at once would be on the order of that floor squared (under 1%) — far below the observed 72.3% co-occurrence among already-matched functions" | Medium ("arithmetic on already-published counts, not a fresh independent measurement") | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`binary-overlap.tsv`) | `allods2.exe` and `a2server.exe` share 4,312 of 5,575 distinct normHash values with each other directly (77.35%, all instruction counts; 65.2%/60.7% restricted to instrCount≥51) — independent of any relationship to ROM1. The two binaries' own matches are not independent events, so no per-binary floor may be squared to estimate their joint rate; the calculation is invalid regardless of which floor is used. The co-occurrence fact itself reproduces under the corrected classification and a broader population (75.4% of the full instrCount≥51 rom.exe population matching either ROM2 binary also matches both, 510/676) and is better explained directly: the two binaries already share most of their own compiled code | **REFUTED** — the independence assumption behind squaring a floor is false for these two binaries |
| `R2-ENGINE-008` (the "three large ones… classify absent" anchor list only; the Human-constructor identical match, the cell-record non-resolution and the literal-search table stand) | Verbatim: "of five cited by standing claims, three large ones (session Serialize `FUN_00539310`, 132 instr.; campaign writer `FUN_00489270`, 279 instr.; campaign reader `FUN_00489580`, 700 instr.) classify absent — no ROM2 function shares their normHash, nor any referenced string at a compatible size" | Medium ("instrCount ≥ 51, same floor as R2-ENGINE-005/006") | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`record-sizes.tsv`) | Under the strict mnemHash+instrCount+byteLen instrument, session Serialize `FUN_00539310` is not absent: it is a mnem-match in both `allods2.exe` (`0057e6c6`) and `a2server.exe` (`005b0556`) — same mnemHash, same 132-instruction count, same 462-byte length, operand values changed at most of its twelve small-immediate positions (as a set, 6 of 12 values recur somewhere in the ROM2 candidate's own list, 6 do not). The campaign writer and reader remain absent under the same strict instrument — no ROM2 function shares their mnemHash+instrCount+byteLen either — so the anchor list narrows from three absent to two | **PARTIALLY RETRACTED** — one of the three named absences is a same-skeleton match with changed operands, not an absence; the other two stand |
| `R2-ENGINE-009` (the per-binary new-code function counts and byte totals only; the per-binary ranking and the string-keyword clustering shape stand) | Verbatim: "a2server.exe: 3,657/8,374 non-thunk functions (43.7%), 1,319,223 bytes. allods2.exe: 3,328/8,875 (37.5%), 1,031,216 bytes. scenario.dll: 170/1,273 (13.4%), 23,631 bytes. engine32.dll: 96/117 (82.1%), 15,323 bytes" | High ("complete population, exact") | [EXP-2001 correction pass](../experiments/EXP-2001-engine-sharing/) (`new-code-recovery.tsv`) | The strict mnemHash+instrCount+byteLen instrument recovers a same-skeleton rom.exe match for some of what the normHash/string-only pass counted as new: 388 of a2server.exe's 3,657 (10.6%), 494 of allods2.exe's 3,328 (14.8%), 13 of scenario.dll's 170 (7.6%), 2 of engine32.dll's 96 (2.1%). The corrected new-code counts are a2server.exe 3,269/8,374 (39.0%), allods2.exe 2,834/8,875 (31.9%), scenario.dll 157/1,273 (12.3%), engine32.dll 94/117 (80.3%) — every binary's share is smaller than originally published; the ranking is unchanged | **NARROWED** — the original counts were accurate under the coarser instrument; the finer one shrinks every one of them |

## Trailer-toggle direct-caller correction

| Claim | Former wording | Confidence when believed | Evidence | Correction | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SAV-647` (the "0 direct E8 callers" clause only; the two toggle sites, their paired on/off strings and the four `Script:`-prefixed consumer sites stand) | Verbatim: "`-callers` finds 0 direct E8 callers for either toggle routine's own containing function; they are reached indirectly and no caller is asserted here" | High — the caller sentence carried the row's own High grade, with no separate search recorded behind it | [EXP-0315](../experiments/EXP-0315-world-head-trailer/) (`SAV-694`, `evidence/closure/references.tsv`, `evidence/closure/decoder-audit.tsv`) | The candidate's own `references.tsv` row `0053cdc0  004d7441  UNCONDITIONAL_CALL  004d5dd8` is a direct `E8` call (`e8 7a 59 06 00` at `004d7441`) into `0053cdc0`, the function `decoder-audit.tsv` attributes both toggle sites to (`0053ceb5`/`0053ced1`/`0053cf22` for `+0x4`, `0053cf6a`/`0053cf84`/`0053cfd4` for `+0x0`). `go run ./tools/cursorsurf -callers 53cdc0` against the preserved EN install returns 1 direct E8 caller, `004d7441` in `004d5e2a`; `-fnof 53cf70` and `-fnof 53cebb` agree. The number was never measured: `EXP-0312`'s own `experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/evidence/regen.sh:135` emits the "0 direct E8 callers" sentence as literal `echo` text, and that script's six real `-callers` invocations (lines 145, 164, 194, 202, 206, 225) are for `4d303e`, `5394e0`, `53b870`, `539be0`, `4e3591` and `53bf10` — none of them `53cdc0`, `53cf70` or `53cebb` | **REFUTED** — the search the sentence describes was never run, and the candidate's own evidence shows one direct caller |
